plan.md
# all-analysis-html

## Decisions
- Build: stdlib Python script rebuilds whole HTML from files each run; add = regenerate. Why: user choice; no drift, no tokens.
- Purpose: results view only, not chronology. One page user opens after each analysis to see new result in context of the rest. Sparse notes (some interpretation kept; user interprets). Why: ANALYSIS_LOG.md covers when/who; report.html covers agent activity; this covers results.
- Separate from report.html (agent-activity dashboard) and ANALYSIS_LOG.md (chronology). Why: user.
- Grouping: section per poe task; inside, analyses nested by `Follows from` chain. Why: existing fields only; new result sits under the one it follows.
- Location: `poe/docs/all_analysis.html`, beside ANALYSIS_LOG.md. Why: project-wide poe file; root stays clean.
- Figures: inline `<img>` with path relative to `poe/docs/`; plus links to notebook + record. Why: page must show results; stays small. Accepted: breaks if files moved.
- Triggers: orchestrator rebuilds after every analysis (analyse step 5), `note`, and catch-up; new mode `results` rebuilds on demand (creates page if absent). Why: page always current; explicit cleanup when wanted.
- Untracked work shown, in context where possible: log entry with `Follows: <slug>/NN` → nested under that analysis; else notebook belongs to a task's notebook path → that task's section; else fallback "Untracked work" section. `note`/catch-up fill `Follows` when inferable (cells sit after a poe-tagged group, or user names it). Why: user — no holes, context over separation.
- Confirmed by user 2026-11-20.

## Objective

One project-wide **results page** (`poe/docs/all_analysis.html`) the user opens after each analysis to see every result, new ones in context. Script-built, always regenerable.

## Scope

- New `skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py` (stdlib only, Python 3.9+, like `poe_report.py`).
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: new `results` mode; rebuild call in Analyse step 5, Note mode, Catch-up check; `note`/catch-up fill `Follows` when inferable; Files section lists `all_analysis.html`.
- `agents/phase-worker.md`: only if record format needs a tweak for parsing (e.g. figure paths one per line under `## Result`).
- `tests/test_poe_results.py` + fixture under `tests/fixtures/`.
- `README.md`: document results page + `results` mode.

Excluded:
- Changes to `report.html` / `poe_report.py`.
- Base64-embedded figures, JavaScript, external resources.
- Topic tags, cross-task grouping, dates/chronology on the page.
- Rendering notebook outputs directly (only saved figures named in records/log).

## Assumptions

- Input: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` + every `poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-*.md` (skip `poe/docs/`, `poe/trash/`). Page built from these only.
- Per analysis shown: question, status, `## Result` text, first paragraph of `## Interpretation` (sparse), inline images, links to record + notebook#heading. Caveats and Next omitted.
- Figures = paths in `## Result` / log `Links` ending `.png .jpg .jpeg .svg .gif`; other paths (csv, pdf) shown as links. Paths resolved relative to session dir, emitted relative to `poe/docs/`. Missing file → link marked missing, no crash.
- Newest = last entry in `ANALYSIS_LOG.md`; marked "latest" and linked from top of page.
- Thread order: roots (no/unknown `Follows`) in NN order; children nested under parent in NN order. Unknown parent → treated as root.
- Untracked placement: `Follows` → under that analysis; else notebook path under a task plan's notebook path → task section; else "Untracked work" section.
- Abandoned/failed analyses shown, dimmed, so threads stay intact.
- Missing log or no analyses → page still written with an empty-state line.
- Page: single self-contained HTML, inline CSS, light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme`, no JS.
- Malformed record/log entry → skipped and counted in page footer, never fatal (as `poe_report.py`).

## Approach

- `poe_results.py <session-dir>` (default `.`): Load (parse log entries + records) → Arrange (tasks → thread trees, untracked placement) → Render (write `poe/docs/all_analysis.html`, print path). Overwrites every run = add and cleanup in one.
- SKILL.md:
  - Mode `results`: run script; reply path only. Allowed writes: `poe/docs/all_analysis.html`.
  - Analyse step 5, Note mode, Catch-up check: run script after log write. Failure → say so, never blocks (as Report).
  - Note mode / catch-up: set `Follows: <slug>/NN` when cells follow a poe-tagged group or user names it.
- Reuse small helpers from `poe_report.py` only by copy if trivial; no shared module unless obviously cleaner.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py <fixture>` writes `poe/docs/all_analysis.html` with: task sections, nested thread, inline img for existing figure, missing-figure marker, untracked entry placed under its `Follows` analysis, one in fallback section, latest marked.
- Second run on same input → byte-identical output.
- No-log / no-analysis dir → page with empty-state, exit 0.
- SKILL.md documents `results` mode and the auto-rebuild points; README updated.
- Existing tests still pass.

## Risks

- Record/log are free-form markdown written by agents → parsing brittle. Mitigation: parse only headings + the fixed log bullet keys; tolerate gaps.
- Relative image paths break if `poe/docs/` or figures moved. Accepted (Decisions).
- Large projects: many images on one page → slow load. Accepted for now; `loading="lazy"` on img.

## Validation

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual: run script on fixture, open HTML, check grouping/figures in light + dark.

# Phase 01: results page

Depends on: nothing. Units 01a and 01b run in parallel (no shared files).

## Shared interface (both units rely on it; do not change)

- Command: `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_results.py" [session-dir]` (default `.`).
- Writes `<session-dir>/poe/docs/all_analysis.html` (creates `poe/docs/` if absent), overwrites every run, prints the path, exit 0 (also when no log / no analyses).
- Inputs: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` + `poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-*.md` for every slug except `docs`, `trash`.
- Figure/table paths in records (`## Result`) and log `Links:` are relative to session dir; plain, in backticks, or as markdown links `[x](path)` all accepted.
- Untracked (`manual`) entry placement: `Follows: <slug>/NN` → under that analysis; else its notebook path matches (same file, else same directory) a notebook named in a task's records → that task's section; else "Untracked work" section.

## Unit 01a: script + tests

- Goal: implement `skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py` per plan Assumptions/Approach + shared interface; fixture + tests.
- Inputs: `poe/all-analysis-html/plan.md` (Decisions, Assumptions, Acceptance criteria), this brief, `skills/poe/SKILL.md` sections "Analysis record" + "Analysis log entry" (formats parsed), `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (style: stdlib, dataclasses, Load/Arrange/Render, defensive parsing, inline CSS, light/dark), `tests/test_poe_report.py` (test style), `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`.
- May change: `skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py` (new), `tests/test_poe_results.py` (new), `tests/fixtures/results/` (new fixture session dir: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`, 2 tasks with records incl. a Follows chain, one failed/abandoned record, one existing small PNG figure, one missing figure, one manual entry with Follows, one manual entry placed by notebook, one manual entry in fallback, one malformed entry).
- Non-goals: no edits to `poe_report.py`, SKILL.md, README, phase-worker.md; no JS, no base64 images, no dates on page, no third-party deps.
- Verify:
  - `python3 -m unittest discover tests` → all pass (old + new).

- Progress notes: `poe/all-analysis-html/progress/01a-script.md`.

## Done so far

- Tests `tests/test_poe_results.py` (23 tests): arrange on fixture (read-only), render on temp copy (shutil.copytree), main empty dir / missing dir.
## Files changed or produced
- skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py (new)
- tests/test_poe_results.py (new)
- tests/fixtures/results/** (new)
## Tests run and observed results
- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` (3.12.11): Ran 67 tests, OK. `/usr/bin/python3` (3.9.6): Ran 67, OK.
- `python3 skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py tests/fixtures/results` twice: printed path, shasum identical (06a6405f...). Generated file removed afterwards (not committed, no .gitignore change).
- Empty scratch dir: exit 0, page "No analyses yet...".
- Not done: visual check in browser light/dark.
## Decisions and assumptions
- Record status = last `: word` of title only if done/failed/abandoned.
- Record `Follows from`: record path `analysis/NN-`, `slug/NN`, or leading bare NN (same task); else root.
- Poe Follows accepted only if same slug and parent NN < child NN (else root): no cycles.
- Log entry slug/NN without record -> analysis built from log entry. Log Links figures/files/notebook merged into the record's.
- Untracked: Follows to known analysis -> child; else notebook same file (any task, slug order) then same dir -> task root; else Untracked work. Roots/children: poe by NN, then manual by log order.
- Paths: plain/backtick/markdown target; token is path if `[\w.~/-]+.ext` and contains "/" or known extension; `#fragment` dropped. Hrefs `../../<path>` URL-quoted; missing files (figures, record, notebook, other) -> `class="missing"` link + "(missing)".
- Notebook link shows path only (heading after `#` not shown).
- Anchors: `analysis-<slug>-<NN>`, `manual-<k>` (k = position among valid log entries). Footer always shows entry/record counts + skipped count.
- Session dir not a directory -> exit 1 (stderr). Write failure -> exit 1.
## Next step / remaining work
- None for 01a. Optional: eyeball page in browser light/dark.

## Operating modes

- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
- `grill <task | plan-path>`: interview the user on open decisions, then write or update `plan.md` (see Grill mode). May write only `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`, and `poe/docs/adr/`; never implementation files.
- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/` (never `poe/docs/` or `poe/trash/`); otherwise ask for the path.
- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9. Task with analyses → also read the latest `analysis/` record and continue the Analyse loop (step 3).
- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
- `analyse [task | plan-path]`: run analyses one at a time on a task, user picks each next one (see Analyse mode). May write `poe/<slug>/` files, `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`, and (via workers) notebooks/outputs under the plan's project path; no other implementation files.
- `analyse catch-up`: catch-up check only (see Catch-up check). May write only `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `poe:manual` cell tags.
- `note <text>`: log untracked work in the user's words (see Note mode). May write only `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `poe:manual` cell tags.
- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.

## Files

Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug. Slug `docs` is reserved.

```
poe/docs/              shared across tasks, created only when first needed (written by `grill`, `analyse`, `note`)
  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
  ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide analysis log, date order, all tasks
poe/<short-task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
  analysis/NN-name.md  one record per analysis (written by `analyse` workers)
  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
```

Notebooks, figures, and intermediates live outside `poe/`, in the project path named in the task's `plan.md` (e.g. `notebooks/<slug>/`); analysis records link to them.

## Plan mode: write `plan.md`

1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context. Also read `poe/docs/` if present.
2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision. Analysis task → plan also gives: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment (incl. how to call `jupyter`), first analysis (`First analysis`).
3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
4. Open decisions (the user's to make, not settleable from the code or sensible defaults) → do not guess; switch to Grill mode with the task. Otherwise write the plan in one shot.
5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
   ````
   Next:
   ```
   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   ```
   ````

## Grill mode: interview, then write `plan.md`

1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `/Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/8105801365da/skills/poe/GRILLING.md`; follow GRILLING.md for questions and writes.
2. Argument is an existing `plan.md` → load it; grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise → new slug and a `plan.md` holding only `## Decisions`.
3. Ask one question, then stop. After the answer, write as GRILLING.md says, then ask the next.
4. Frontier empty → ask the user to confirm shared understanding.
5. Confirmed → write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5.
   `Next:` command: analysis-only task → `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`; coding or mixed → `split <plan path>`, mixed adds one reply bullet: `analyse <plan path>` works after execute.
6. Copy the logs (see Logs) at the end of the mode.

## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units

Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.

1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. When `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant `poe/docs/adr/` files exist, list them in each unit's inputs. Analysis unit → brief also gives notebook path + record path; units that write `ANALYSIS_LOG.md` run sequentially.
5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
   ````
   Next:
   ```
   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   ```
   ````

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output. Analysis unit → also notebook path, record path, log path; worker follows Notebook rules and writes the record + log entry besides its progress notes; log-writing units run one at a time.
4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.

## Analyse mode

One analysis at a time; user picks each next one. `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.

1. No plan → as Plan step 4: open decisions → Grill mode with the task; else write short `plan.md` (Plan step 2 analysis fields). Either way stop for review as Plan step 5, `Next:` block holding `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`.
   Plan exists, analysis fields (Plan step 2) missing → look up facts; grill the rest (GRILLING.md method, `## Decisions` appended); write fields into `plan.md`; continue. Else → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` (as Execute step 2) with: question for this analysis (first: plan's `First analysis`; later: user's pick), notebook path, record path `analysis/NN-name.md`, previous record path, log path `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error (no `"output_type": "error"`) and record exists; update `status.md`; ask next analysis via `AskUserQuestion` (worker's options + done); write the chosen option into the record's `## Next`; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.
7. User says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.

## Analysis record

`analysis/NN-name.md`, written by the worker:

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

## Analysis log entry

Appended to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` (create with `# Analysis log` heading if absent):

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

## Notebook rules

- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata: `tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`. Untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.
- Execute headless: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`, in the plan's environment; `jupyter` missing → report and stop. Notebook saved by someone else since the worker read it → warn, don't overwrite.

## Catch-up check

- Runs at start of every `analyse`; also `analyse catch-up` alone.
- Scan notebooks under the plan's notebook/output path for untagged code cells; a notebook with no tagged cells at all counts as one group.
- Per group of adjacent untagged cells: draft `manual` log entry from code + outputs, ask user to confirm/fix wording, append. Tag cells `poe:manual` so they are not re-asked.

## Note mode

- Append `manual` log entry: user's text as `<question>`, `Result` only if the text states one; link notebook if named or unambiguous; tag matching untagged cells `poe:manual`. No worker. Reply: entry path only.
- Logs copied into the active task if unambiguous; otherwise skipped.

## Logs

At the end of every mode (`plan`, `grill`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`, `analyse`, `note`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:

```sh
dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/5619ec58-9234-4413-bc5d-9cb3592b7d9b"
src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "5619ec58-9234-4413-bc5d-9cb3592b7d9b.jsonl" | head -1)
mkdir -p "$dest"
cp "$src" "$dest/"
[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
```

- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.

## Report

At the end of `execute` and `resume` (after `summary.md` and the log copy), and in `report` mode, build `report.html` from the task directory:

```sh
python3 "/Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/8105801365da/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<short-task-slug>
```

- The script writes `poe/<short-task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. Re-running it replaces the earlier report.
- If it fails (for example, `python3` is missing or the command is refused), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing report never blocks the task.

## Progress notes

Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.

```
# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
## Done so far
## Files changed or produced
## Tests run and observed results
## Decisions and assumptions
## Next step / remaining work
```

When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.

## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent

- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.

## Constraints

- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

# Grilling method Interview method for `grill` mode: settle every open decision with the user, recording terms and hard decisions as you go.show the rest (10,996 more characters)
Adapted from the `grilling` and `domain-modeling` skills by Matt Pocock: https://github.com/mattpocock/skills

> MIT License. Copyright (c) 2026 Matt Pocock.
> Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy of this software and associated documentation files (the "Software"), to deal in the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do so, subject to the following conditions:
> The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.
> THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.

## Decision tree

- Task = tree of decisions. A decision depends on others when its options only make sense once they are settled.
- **Frontier** = open decisions whose prerequisites are all settled. Ask one frontier decision at a time; deeper questions wait until their prerequisites are answered.
- `grill <plan-path>`: tree = the plan's assumptions, open points, risky points. Settled `## Decisions` entries are not re-asked.

## Facts vs decisions

- Facts (what the code does, what exists, versions, conventions, test setup): look them up in the code, or with read-only exploration subagents. Never ask the user.
- Decisions (what the user wants, trade-offs only they can weigh): ask.
- Missing detail with an obvious default = not a question. Use the default; note it under Assumptions.

## Analysis tasks

- Applies when the request is data analysis, or grill was entered from `analyse`.
- Tree includes the five analysis fields: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment + `jupyter` call, first analysis (`First analysis`).
- Facts among them (where the data is, which environments exist, whether `jupyter` is available): look up, never ask.
- Fields already in the plan: not re-asked.

## Questions

- Ask exactly one question per turn: the frontier decision that most other decisions depend on.
- Question counter:
  - Before Q1, estimate N = all open decisions known so far (whole tree, not just frontier), excl. final confirm. `grill <plan-path>` → only the open points being grilled.
  - Prefix every `question`: `Q<k> of ~<N>: `. `~` always shown. `header` unchanged.
  - After each answer, re-estimate N (asked + still open); show the new N on the next question. k +1 per question, never resets within a run; new run after `/clear` restarts at Q1.
  - Final "Matches your understanding?" confirm: no prefix, not counted.
- Ask it with `AskUserQuestion`, one question per call. Never batch questions.
  - `question`: terse, self-contained (the dialog may be all the user sees); include any finding it depends on.
  - `header`: decision label, ≤12 chars.
  - Options: 2–4 concrete, mutually exclusive answers. Recommended first, label ends `(Recommended)`, `description` = short why. Other options' `description` = trade-off.
  - No manual "Other" option (added automatically). `multiSelect` only when answers genuinely combine.
  - `preview` only when comparing concrete artifacts (layouts, snippets).
- Open-ended decision (no sensible option list, e.g. naming a term) → still the dialog: 2 best candidates as options; user types own via "Other".
- Tool unavailable or call rejected → fall back to plain text: one question (same `Q<k> of ~<N>: ` prefix) + `Rec:` line, then stop.
- Pick the next question only after the answer; an answer may add, remove, or change later questions.
- Recommendations follow SKILL.md Plan step 3 "Do not overengineer": simplest option meeting the stated need, repo's existing tools/patterns, no speculative features or config.
- Skip trivia: no question whose answer would not change the plan. A clear task may need one question or none.

## Domain modeling

While grilling, sharpen the language:
- Term used loosely, or two words for one thing → ask which one and what exactly it means.
- Probe a term with a concrete edge-case scenario ("note edited after archiving: still archived?").
- Check terms against existing code names and `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`; flag conflicts.

## Writing after every answer

After each answer, before asking the next question:
1. Append the settled decision to `## Decisions` in `poe/<slug>/plan.md` (one bullet: decision + short why).
2. Add or update glossary entries for terms the answer resolved.
3. Write an ADR for any decision that qualifies (below).

Nothing settled lives only in the chat; `/clear` then `grill <plan-path>` must resume without re-asking.

## Glossary

- Path: `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`. Create only when the first term is resolved.
- Project-specific terms only. No generic programming words. No implementation detail (file names, classes, schemas).
- Entry format:
  ```
  **Term**: 1–2 sentence definition.
  _Avoid_: synonym, other synonym
  ```
- Keep entries alphabetical; edit an existing entry instead of adding a duplicate.

## ADRs

- Path: `poe/docs/adr/NNNN-slug.md`. Create `adr/` only when the first ADR is written.
- Write one only when all three hold:
  1. Hard to reverse (costly to change later).
  2. Surprising (a future reader would ask "why this way?").
  3. Real trade-off (viable alternatives were rejected).
- Otherwise the decision lives only in `plan.md` `## Decisions`. Zero ADRs is normal.
- Number = highest existing number + 1, 4 digits (`0001` if none). Slug = short kebab-case title.
- Format:
  ```
  # NNNN: <Title>

  <1–3 sentences: context, decision, why.>
  ```
  Add sections (Considered options, Consequences) only when they add real value.

## Finish

- Frontier empty → summarize understanding in ≤5 bullets as plain text, then `AskUserQuestion` "Matches your understanding?": `Yes, write the plan (Recommended)` / `Needs correction`.
- Correction → ask what in plain text; re-ask the affected decisions, one at a time.
- Confirmed → grilling done; continue Grill mode in SKILL.md.

# Glossary **Analysis**: One unit of data-analysis work in a poe task: a question, the notebook cells run to answer it, the result, its interpretation, and the next analysis chosen. Recorded in one file per analysis.show the rest (7,333 more characters)
_Avoid_: analysis step, step, finding

**Step**: One event in an agent transcript as shown in the report (a message, tool call, or tool result).
_Avoid_: using "step" for a unit of analysis work

**Untracked work**: Analysis done outside poe, by the user or by an agent not using poe. Enters the analysis log as a `manual` entry through a note or the catch-up check.
_Avoid_: hand-written work, manual work

# poe-analysis

## Decisions
- Work shape: both. New step-loop mode for exploratory analysis (one step, record, user picks next) + existing plan/split/execute for well-defined analyses. Why: exploration must react to data; fixed analyses still benefit from batch.
- Code home: agent-run notebook. Agent adds cells to a .ipynb and executes headless; outputs stay in notebook; user opens same file. Why: matches existing Jupyter workflow, no extra tooling.
- Switching: one task, mixed. Single poe/<slug>/ holds analysis steps and coding phases; one plan, status, report. Switch = run other mode on same dir. Why: one story, nothing to link.
- Step record: step file + notebook. poe/<slug>/steps/NN-name.md per step (question, what run, result, interpretation, next step) pointing to notebook cells; notebook gets short markdown heading per step. Why: poe files drive resume/report; notebook readable alone.
- Notebook layout: agent decides per task. Start one notebook; start a new one + save intermediates when full re-runs get slow. Why: user choice; avoids forcing many files on small analyses. Plan must state a clear rule for when to split.
- Step runner: fresh worker per step. Subagent writes cells, runs, writes step file, returns short result. Why: keeps main session small; same pattern as execute.
- File home: project path fixed in plan.md (e.g. notebooks/<slug>/); step files in poe/<slug>/ link to notebooks/outputs. Why: deliverables where collaborators expect; large data out of poe/.
- Report: step files rendered as text + links to notebooks/figures; nothing embedded. Why (user): report.html tracks what the agent did, not outcomes.
- Naming: unit of analysis work = "analysis" (not "analysis step"); records in poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md. Mode name `analyse` (verb, like other modes) = my interpretation, to confirm. Why: avoids clash with report "step" (transcript event).
- Timeline: one project-wide poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md, worker appends dated entry per analysis (question, one-line result, links to record/notebook/figures, which analysis it follows). Why: months of analyses across tasks must read as one chronology for the paper. Must not assume all work is agent/poe-done (hand edits: see next).
- Hand edits: both. `note <text>` mode logs user work in own words on demand; catch-up check at start of every `analyse` run (and on demand) finds notebook cells not covered by records, drafts "manual" log entries, user confirms. Why: not all analysis is agent/poe-done; log must have no holes.
- Few files: human must read every bit of analysis. Notebooks kept to as few as organisation and runtime need; new notebook only for a stated reason. Refines "Notebook layout".
- Untracked work = any work done outside poe: by the user or by a non-poe agent (rare). Catch-up and `note` cover both.
- Confirmed by user 2026-11-20, mode name `analyse` included.

## Objective

Let one poe task hold both coding and data analysis, with every analysis recorded and one project-wide chronology usable at paper write-up.

## Scope

- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: new modes `analyse`, `note`; analysis files in Files section; analysis-record format; catch-up check; notebook rules; analysis units in `split`/`execute`.
- `agents/phase-worker.md`: rules for an analysis unit (notebook, tagging, record, log entry).
- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` + `tests/test_poe_report.py`: render `analysis/` records.
- `README.md`: document the above.

Excluded:
- Live-kernel / Jupyter MCP execution.
- Embedding figures or notebooks in `report.html`.
- Entry status (kept / superseded / dead end) in the log.
- Where analysis runs (local vs HPC): per-task, stated in that task's plan.
- R / non-Python notebooks.

## Assumptions

- Headless execution: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`; worker reports and stops if `jupyter` missing.
- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata (`tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`); untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Analysis numbering `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.
- `analyse` loop ends when user says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.
- Next-analysis choice asked via `AskUserQuestion`, worker proposes 2–3 options.
- `resume` on a task with analyses: reads `status.md` + latest analysis record, continues loop.

## Approach

### Files

```
poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide, date order, all tasks
poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md   one record per analysis
<project path from plan.md>/     notebooks, figures, intermediates
```

- Task `plan.md` for analysis work adds: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment.

### `analyse [task | plan-path]`

1. No plan → write short `plan.md` (question, data, paths), stop for review as Plan mode. Plan exists → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` with: question for this analysis, notebook path, record path, previous record path, log path.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error and record exists; update `status.md`; ask user for next analysis; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.

### Analysis record (`analysis/NN-name.md`)

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

### Notebook rules (worker)

## Facts gathered

- repowise: Python 3.11+ CLI (`uv tool install repowise`), AGPL-3.0; dashboard needs Node 20+.
- `repowise init --yes --no-prose`: graph, git intelligence, health, dead code, change risk; zero LLM calls, no key.
- LLM (own key or Ollama) only for wiki prose, decision mining, refactor codegen.
- Ships MCP server (10 default tools, staleness `_meta`), Claude Code plugin, hooks, CLAUDE.md generation; `repowise update` incremental.
- Source: https://github.com/repowise-dev/repowise (README, fetched 2026-11-19; not installed or tested locally).
- poe today: SKILL.md + one stdlib-only script (`poe_report.py`, 871 lines), Python 3.9+, no dependencies.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build a step-by-step report for a poe task directory.

Usage: python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>

Three parts: Load reads a task's documents and Claude Code transcripts from
poe/<task-slug>/, Timeline turns them into plain dataclasses, and Render
writes them as one self-contained poe/<task-slug>/report.html (inline CSS,
no JavaScript, no external resources). Python 3 standard library only.

The transcript format is undocumented, so every field is read defensively.
Lines that are not valid JSON, records of unknown type and records without
the expected fields are skipped and counted, never fatal.
"""

import argparse
import base64
import html
import json
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Optional, Set, Tuple

# Record types Claude Code writes for its own bookkeeping. They are ignored
# without being counted as skipped; any other unknown type is counted.
INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES = {
    "attachment",
    "api-request",
    "api-request-blob",
    "api-request-shape",
    "mode",
    "session-owner",
    "atis-latch",
    "last-prompt",
    "ai-title",
    "permission-mode",
    "tools-used",
    "queue-operation",
}
INTERNAL_RECORD_PREFIXES = ("file-history-",)

TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES = ["plan.md", "status.md", "summary.md"]
TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS = ["phases", "progress", "analysis"]

# Only the start of a thinking block's signature is decoded (signatures can be
# several KB). Must be a multiple of 4 so the cut prefix is valid base64.
SIGNATURE_PREFIX_CHARS = 64


@dataclass
class Step:
    index: int
    timestamp: Optional[str]
    kind: str  # user, assistant, thinking, narration, tool_call, tool_result, system
    text: str
    anchor: str
    tool_name: Optional[str] = None
    tool_use_id: Optional[str] = None
45:TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS = ["phases", "progress", "analysis"]

#47 · 2026-11-20T17:59:55.076Z · tool result · Bash
# Glossary **Analysis**: One unit of data-analysis work in a poe task: a question, the notebook cells run to answer it, the result, its interpretation, and the next analysis chosen. Recorded in one file per analysis.show the rest (661 more characters)
_Avoid_: analysis step, step, finding

**Results page**: Project-wide page showing every analysis result (poe and untracked) grouped by task and thread, rebuilt from the analysis log and records. Not a chronology and not an agent-activity report.
_Avoid_: dashboard, report, all-analysis page

**Step**: One event in an agent transcript as shown in the report (a message, tool call, or tool result).
_Avoid_: using "step" for a unit of analysis work

**Untracked work**: Analysis done outside poe, by the user or by an agent not using poe. Enters the analysis log as a `manual` entry through a note or the catch-up check.
_Avoid_: hand-written work, manual work

#56 · 2026-11-20T18:00:48.798Z · assistant
The plan for the results page is written: `poe/all-analysis-html/plan.md`.

- **What gets built:** a new script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py`, that writes `poe/docs/all_analysis.html` from `ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and the analysis records. Every run rebuilds the whole page, so adding a result and cleaning up the page are the same call. If the page doesn't exist yet, the script creates it.
- **Layout:** one section per task, with analyses nested under the one they follow. Each shows its question, result, a short interpretation, its figures inline, and links to the notebook and record. The newest is marked "latest". Dates and agent activity stay in `ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `report.html`.
- **Untracked work:** placed under the analysis it follows, or else in its task's section. Anything that can't be placed goes in an "Untracked work" section. `note` and the catch-up check will fill in the `Follows` field when they can tell which analysis came before.
- **When it rebuilds:** automatically after each analysis, `note` and catch-up, plus a new `/poe:poe results` mode to rebuild on demand.
- **Also added:** a "Results page" entry in `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`. No architecture decision record was needed.

Next:
```
/poe:poe split poe/all-analysis-html/plan.md
```

#60 · 2026-11-20T18:01:08.114Z · user
Base directory for this skill: /Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/8105801365da/skills/poe # Phase-Orchestrated Work User request: split poe/all-analysis-html/plan.md You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context. ## Output style Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision. show the rest (19,373 more characters)
- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
- Bullets and fragments over prose.
- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.

## Operating modes

- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
- `grill <task | plan-path>`: interview the user on open decisions, then write or update `plan.md` (see Grill mode). May write only `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`, and `poe/docs/adr/`; never implementation files.
- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/` (never `poe/docs/` or `poe/trash/`); otherwise ask for the path.
- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9. Task with analyses → also read the latest `analysis/` record and continue the Analyse loop (step 3).
- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
- `analyse [task | plan-path]`: run analyses one at a time on a task, user picks each next one (see Analyse mode). May write `poe/<slug>/` files, `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`, and (via workers) notebooks/outputs under the plan's project path; no other implementation files.
- `analyse catch-up`: catch-up check only (see Catch-up check). May write only `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `poe:manual` cell tags.
- `note <text>`: log untracked work in the user's words (see Note mode). May write only `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `poe:manual` cell tags.
- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.

## Files

Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug. Slug `docs` is reserved.

```
poe/docs/              shared across tasks, created only when first needed (written by `grill`, `analyse`, `note`)
  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
  ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide analysis log, date order, all tasks
poe/<short-task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
  analysis/NN-name.md  one record per analysis (written by `analyse` workers)
  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
```

Notebooks, figures, and intermediates live outside `poe/`, in the project path named in the task's `plan.md` (e.g. `notebooks/<slug>/`); analysis records link to them.

## Plan mode: write `plan.md`

1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context. Also read `poe/docs/` if present.
2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision. Analysis task → plan also gives: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment (incl. how to call `jupyter`), first analysis (`First analysis`).
3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
4. Open decisions (the user's to make, not settleable from the code or sensible defaults) → do not guess; switch to Grill mode with the task. Otherwise write the plan in one shot.
5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
   ````
   Next:
   ```
   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   ```
   ````

## Grill mode: interview, then write `plan.md`

1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `/Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/8105801365da/skills/poe/GRILLING.md`; follow GRILLING.md for questions and writes.
2. Argument is an existing `plan.md` → load it; grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise → new slug and a `plan.md` holding only `## Decisions`.
3. Ask one question, then stop. After the answer, write as GRILLING.md says, then ask the next.
4. Frontier empty → ask the user to confirm shared understanding.
5. Confirmed → write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5.
   `Next:` command: analysis-only task → `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`; coding or mixed → `split <plan path>`, mixed adds one reply bullet: `analyse <plan path>` works after execute.
6. Copy the logs (see Logs) at the end of the mode.

## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units

Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.

1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. When `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant `poe/docs/adr/` files exist, list them in each unit's inputs. Analysis unit → brief also gives notebook path + record path; units that write `ANALYSIS_LOG.md` run sequentially.
5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
   ````
   Next:
   ```
   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   ```
   ````

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output. Analysis unit → also notebook path, record path, log path; worker follows Notebook rules and writes the record + log entry besides its progress notes; log-writing units run one at a time.
4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.

## Analyse mode

One analysis at a time; user picks each next one. `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.

1. No plan → as Plan step 4: open decisions → Grill mode with the task; else write short `plan.md` (Plan step 2 analysis fields). Either way stop for review as Plan step 5, `Next:` block holding `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`.
   Plan exists, analysis fields (Plan step 2) missing → look up facts; grill the rest (GRILLING.md method, `## Decisions` appended); write fields into `plan.md`; continue. Else → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` (as Execute step 2) with: question for this analysis (first: plan's `First analysis`; later: user's pick), notebook path, record path `analysis/NN-name.md`, previous record path, log path `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error (no `"output_type": "error"`) and record exists; update `status.md`; ask next analysis via `AskUserQuestion` (worker's options + done); write the chosen option into the record's `## Next`; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.
7. User says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.

## Analysis record

`analysis/NN-name.md`, written by the worker:

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

## Analysis log entry

Appended to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` (create with `# Analysis log` heading if absent):

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

## Notebook rules

- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata: `tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`. Untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.
- Execute headless: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`, in the plan's environment; `jupyter` missing → report and stop. Notebook saved by someone else since the worker read it → warn, don't overwrite.

## Catch-up check

- Runs at start of every `analyse`; also `analyse catch-up` alone.
- Scan notebooks under the plan's notebook/output path for untagged code cells; a notebook with no tagged cells at all counts as one group.
- Per group of adjacent untagged cells: draft `manual` log entry from code + outputs, ask user to confirm/fix wording, append. Tag cells `poe:manual` so they are not re-asked.

## Note mode

- Append `manual` log entry: user's text as `<question>`, `Result` only if the text states one; link notebook if named or unambiguous; tag matching untagged cells `poe:manual`. No worker. Reply: entry path only.
- Logs copied into the active task if unambiguous; otherwise skipped.

## Logs

At the end of every mode (`plan`, `grill`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`, `analyse`, `note`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:

```sh
dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/5619ec58-9234-4413-bc5d-9cb3592b7d9b"
src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "5619ec58-9234-4413-bc5d-9cb3592b7d9b.jsonl" | head -1)
mkdir -p "$dest"
cp "$src" "$dest/"
[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
```

- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.

## Report

At the end of `execute` and `resume` (after `summary.md` and the log copy), and in `report` mode, build `report.html` from the task directory:

```sh
python3 "/Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/8105801365da/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<short-task-slug>
```

- The script writes `poe/<short-task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. Re-running it replaces the earlier report.
- If it fails (for example, `python3` is missing or the command is refused), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing report never blocks the task.

## Progress notes

Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.

```
# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
## Done so far
## Files changed or produced
## Tests run and observed results
## Decisions and assumptions
## Next step / remaining work
```

When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.

## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent

- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.

## Constraints

- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

## Objective

One project-wide **results page** (`poe/docs/all_analysis.html`) the user opens after each analysis to see every result, new ones in context. Script-built, always regenerable.

## Scope

- New `skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py` (stdlib only, Python 3.9+, like `poe_report.py`).
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: new `results` mode; rebuild call in Analyse step 5, Note mode, Catch-up check; `note`/catch-up fill `Follows` when inferable; Files section lists `all_analysis.html`.
- `agents/phase-worker.md`: only if record format needs a tweak for parsing (e.g. figure paths one per line under `## Result`).
- `tests/test_poe_results.py` + fixture under `tests/fixtures/`.
- `README.md`: document results page + `results` mode.

Excluded:
- Changes to `report.html` / `poe_report.py`.
- Base64-embedded figures, JavaScript, external resources.
- Topic tags, cross-task grouping, dates/chronology on the page.
- Rendering notebook outputs directly (only saved figures named in records/log).

## Assumptions

- Input: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` + every `poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-*.md` (skip `poe/docs/`, `poe/trash/`). Page built from these only.
- Per analysis shown: question, status, `## Result` text, first paragraph of `## Interpretation` (sparse), inline images, links to record + notebook#heading. Caveats and Next omitted.
- Figures = paths in `## Result` / log `Links` ending `.png .jpg .jpeg .svg .gif`; other paths (csv, pdf) shown as links. Paths resolved relative to session dir, emitted relative to `poe/docs/`. Missing file → link marked missing, no crash.
- Newest = last entry in `ANALYSIS_LOG.md`; marked "latest" and linked from top of page.
- Thread order: roots (no/unknown `Follows`) in NN order; children nested under parent in NN order. Unknown parent → treated as root.
- Untracked placement: `Follows` → under that analysis; else notebook path under a task plan's notebook path → task section; else "Untracked work" section.
- Abandoned/failed analyses shown, dimmed, so threads stay intact.
- Missing log or no analyses → page still written with an empty-state line.
- Page: single self-contained HTML, inline CSS, light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme`, no JS.
- Malformed record/log entry → skipped and counted in page footer, never fatal (as `poe_report.py`).

## Approach

- `poe_results.py <session-dir>` (default `.`): Load (parse log entries + records) → Arrange (tasks → thread trees, untracked placement) → Render (write `poe/docs/all_analysis.html`, print path). Overwrites every run = add and cleanup in one.
- SKILL.md:
  - Mode `results`: run script; reply path only. Allowed writes: `poe/docs/all_analysis.html`.
  - Analyse step 5, Note mode, Catch-up check: run script after log write. Failure → say so, never blocks (as Report).
  - Note mode / catch-up: set `Follows: <slug>/NN` when cells follow a poe-tagged group or user names it.
- Reuse small helpers from `poe_report.py` only by copy if trivial; no shared module unless obviously cleaner.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 skills/poe/scripts/poe_results.py <fixture>` writes `poe/docs/all_analysis.html` with: task sections, nested thread, inline img for existing figure, missing-figure marker, untracked entry placed under its `Follows` analysis, one in fallback section, latest marked.
- Second run on same input → byte-identical output.
- No-log / no-analysis dir → page with empty-state, exit 0.
- SKILL.md documents `results` mode and the auto-rebuild points; README updated.
- Existing tests still pass.

## Risks

- Record/log are free-form markdown written by agents → parsing brittle. Mitigation: parse only headings + the fixed log bullet keys; tolerate gaps.
- Relative image paths break if `poe/docs/` or figures moved. Accepted (Decisions).
- Large projects: many images on one page → slow load. Accepted for now; `loading="lazy"` on img.

## Validation

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual: run script on fixture, open HTML, check grouping/figures in light + dark.
