1 --- 2 name: poe 3 description: Plan, decompose, and execute complex coding or analysis tasks in phases using fresh-context subagents and file-based progress notes. Use when the user explicitly requests staged multi-agent work, context-conscious orchestration, or continuation of an existing phase plan.show the rest (21,400 more characters)
     4	argument-hint: "[plan|grill|split|execute|resume|report|analyse|note] [task description or plan path]"
     5	disable-model-invocation: true
     6	---
     7	
     8	# Phase-Orchestrated Work
     9	
    10	User request: $ARGUMENTS
    11	
    12	You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context.
    13	
    14	## Output style
    15	
    16	Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision.
    17	
    18	- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
    19	- Bullets and fragments over prose.
    20	- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
    21	- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.
    22	
    23	## Operating modes
    24	
    25	- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
    26	- `grill <task | plan-path>`: interview the user on open decisions, then write or update `plan.md` (see Grill mode). May write only `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`, and `poe/docs/adr/`; never implementation files.
    27	- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
    28	- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
    29	- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/` (never `poe/docs/` or `poe/trash/`); otherwise ask for the path.
    30	- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9. Task with analyses → also read the latest `analysis/` record and continue the Analyse loop (step 3).
    31	- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
    32	  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
    33	- `analyse [task | plan-path]`: run analyses one at a time on a task, user picks each next one (see Analyse mode). May write `poe/<slug>/` files, `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`, and (via workers) notebooks/outputs under the plan's project path; no other implementation files.
    34	- `analyse catch-up`: catch-up check only (see Catch-up check). May write only `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `poe:manual` cell tags.
    35	- `note <text>`: log untracked work in the user's words (see Note mode). May write only `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` and `poe:manual` cell tags.
    36	- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.
    37	
    38	## Files
    39	
    40	Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug. Slug `docs` is reserved.
    41	
    42	```
    43	poe/docs/              shared across tasks, created only when first needed (written by `grill`, `analyse`, `note`)
    44	  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
    45	  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
    46	  ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide analysis log, date order, all tasks
    47	poe/<short-task-slug>/
    48	  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
    49	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
    50	  analysis/NN-name.md  one record per analysis (written by `analyse` workers)
    51	  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
    52	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
    53	  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
    54	  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
    55	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    56	    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    57	    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    58	    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
    59	```
    60	
    61	Notebooks, figures, and intermediates live outside `poe/`, in the project path named in the task's `plan.md` (e.g. `notebooks/<slug>/`); analysis records link to them.
    62	
    63	## Plan mode: write `plan.md`
    64	
    65	1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context. Also read `poe/docs/` if present.
    66	2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision. Analysis task → plan also gives: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment (incl. how to call `jupyter`), first analysis (`First analysis`).
    67	3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
    68	   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
    69	   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
    70	   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
    71	   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
    72	4. Open decisions (the user's to make, not settleable from the code or sensible defaults) → do not guess; switch to Grill mode with the task. Otherwise write the plan in one shot.
    73	5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
    74	   ````
    75	   Next:
    76	   ```
    77	   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
    78	   ```
    79	   ````
    80	
    81	## Grill mode: interview, then write `plan.md`
    82	
    83	1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/GRILLING.md`; follow GRILLING.md for questions and writes.
    84	2. Argument is an existing `plan.md` → load it; grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise → new slug and a `plan.md` holding only `## Decisions`.
    85	3. Ask one question, then stop. After the answer, write as GRILLING.md says, then ask the next.
    86	4. Frontier empty → ask the user to confirm shared understanding.
    87	5. Confirmed → write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5.
    88	   `Next:` command: analysis-only task → `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`; coding or mixed → `split <plan path>`, mixed adds one reply bullet: `analyse <plan path>` works after execute.
    89	6. Copy the logs (see Logs) at the end of the mode.
    90	
    91	## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units
    92	
    93	Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.
    94	
    95	1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
    96	2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
    97	3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
    98	4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. When `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant `poe/docs/adr/` files exist, list them in each unit's inputs. Analysis unit → brief also gives notebook path + record path; units that write `ANALYSIS_LOG.md` run sequentially.
    99	5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
   100	6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
   101	   ````
   102	   Next:
   103	   ```
   104	   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   105	   ```
   106	   ````
   107	
   108	## Execute mode
   109	
   110	1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
   111	2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
   112	3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output. Analysis unit → also notebook path, record path, log path; worker follows Notebook rules and writes the record + log entry besides its progress notes; log-writing units run one at a time.
   113	4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
   114	5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
   115	6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
   116	7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
   117	   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
   118	8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
   119	9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
   120	   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
   121	
   122	## Analyse mode
   123	
   124	One analysis at a time; user picks each next one. `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.
   125	
   126	1. No plan → as Plan step 4: open decisions → Grill mode with the task; else write short `plan.md` (Plan step 2 analysis fields). Either way stop for review as Plan step 5, `Next:` block holding `/poe:poe analyse <plan path>`.
   127	   Plan exists, analysis fields (Plan step 2) missing → look up facts; grill the rest (GRILLING.md method, `## Decisions` appended); write fields into `plan.md`; continue. Else → continue.
   128	2. Catch-up check (below).
   129	3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` (as Execute step 2) with: question for this analysis (first: plan's `First analysis`; later: user's pick), notebook path, record path `analysis/NN-name.md`, previous record path, log path `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`.
   130	4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
   131	5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error (no `"output_type": "error"`) and record exists; update `status.md`; ask next analysis via `AskUserQuestion` (worker's options + done); write the chosen option into the record's `## Next`; repeat from 3.
   132	6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.
   133	7. User says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.
   134	
   135	## Analysis record
   136	
   137	`analysis/NN-name.md`, written by the worker:
   138	
   139	```
   140	# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
   141	## Follows from
   142	## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
   143	## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
   144	## Interpretation
   145	## Caveats
   146	## Next                options proposed; option chosen
   147	```
   148	
   149	## Analysis log entry
   150	
   151	Appended to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` (create with `# Analysis log` heading if absent):
   152	
   153	```
   154	## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
   155	- Result: one line
   156	- By: poe | user | other agent
   157	- Follows: <slug>/NN
   158	- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
   159	```
   160	
   161	## Notebook rules
   162	
   163	- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata: `tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`. Untagged code cells = untracked work.
   164	- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
   165	- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
   166	- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.
   167	- Execute headless: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`, in the plan's environment; `jupyter` missing → report and stop. Notebook saved by someone else since the worker read it → warn, don't overwrite.
   168	
   169	## Catch-up check
   170	
   171	- Runs at start of every `analyse`; also `analyse catch-up` alone.
   172	- Scan notebooks under the plan's notebook/output path for untagged code cells; a notebook with no tagged cells at all counts as one group.
   173	- Per group of adjacent untagged cells: draft `manual` log entry from code + outputs, ask user to confirm/fix wording, append. Tag cells `poe:manual` so they are not re-asked.
   174	
   175	## Note mode
   176	
   177	- Append `manual` log entry: user's text as `<question>`, `Result` only if the text states one; link notebook if named or unambiguous; tag matching untagged cells `poe:manual`. No worker. Reply: entry path only.
   178	- Logs copied into the active task if unambiguous; otherwise skipped.
   179	
   180	## Logs
   181	
   182	At the end of every mode (`plan`, `grill`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`, `analyse`, `note`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
   183	
   184	```sh
   185	dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}"
   186	src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}.jsonl" | head -1)
   187	mkdir -p "$dest"
   188	cp "$src" "$dest/"
   189	[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
   190	```
   191	
   192	- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
   193	- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
   194	- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.
   195	
   196	## Report
   197	
   198	At the end of `execute` and `resume` (after `summary.md` and the log copy), and in `report` mode, build `report.html` from the task directory:
   199	
   200	```sh
   201	python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<short-task-slug>
   202	```
   203	
   204	- The script writes `poe/<short-task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. Re-running it replaces the earlier report.
   205	- If it fails (for example, `python3` is missing or the command is refused), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing report never blocks the task.
   206	
   207	## Progress notes
   208	
   209	Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.
   210	
   211	```
   212	# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
   213	## Done so far
   214	## Files changed or produced
   215	## Tests run and observed results
   216	## Decisions and assumptions
   217	## Next step / remaining work
   218	```
   219	
   220	When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.
   221	
   222	## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent
   223	
   224	- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
   225	- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
   226	- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
   227	- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
   228	- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
   229	- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.
   230	
   231	## Constraints
   232	
   233	- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
   234	- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
   235	- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
   236	- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
   237	- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

#15 · 2026-11-20T18:04:51.757Z · tool result · Bash
## Usage Plugin skills are namespaced, so the command is `/poe:poe`:show the rest (10,466 more characters)
```text
/poe:poe plan Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
/poe:poe grill Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
/poe:poe grill poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe split poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe execute poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe resume poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe report poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe analyse poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe analyse catch-up
/poe:poe note Plotted ORF lengths per genotype in notebooks/hpv-orf-analysis/orfs.ipynb
```

- **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it. If it finds open decisions that only you can make, it switches to grill instead of guessing.
- **Grill** interviews you before planning. It asks one question at a time in a dialog: pick an option (the recommended one comes first), or type your own answer under Other. Each question shows `Q1 of ~N`, an estimate revised as answers add or remove questions. `grill <task>` starts from scratch and writes `plan.md` once you confirm; `grill <plan-path>` questions an existing plan's assumptions and open points and updates it in place. Decisions are added to `plan.md` under `## Decisions` after every answer; project terms and hard-to-reverse decisions go to the shared `poe/docs/`. On an analysis task it also settles the analysis fields, including the first analysis, and the reply ends with the `analyse` command instead of `split` (a task mixing coding and analysis still gets `split`, with a note that `analyse` works after execute).
- **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.
- **Execute** hands each work unit to a fresh `poe:phase-worker` subagent, which writes progress notes as it works. The orchestrator verifies each unit before moving on.
- **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.
- **Report** copies the session's logs and (re)builds `report.html`, a step-by-step view of what the orchestrator and each worker did. Execute and resume also build it automatically at the end, after `summary.md` and the log copy; if the build fails, the reply says so and the task carries on.
- **Analyse** runs data analyses one at a time in a Jupyter notebook; you pick each next one. With no plan, or a plan missing analysis fields, it asks you the open decisions first instead of guessing. See [Analysis](#analysis).
- **Analyse catch-up** finds notebook cells poe did not write and logs them, with your confirmation.
- **Note** logs analysis you did yourself, in your own words.

Use `plan` when the task is clear. Use `grill` when you want to be interviewed, or to challenge a draft plan.

Output is deliberately terse: replies point to files instead of repeating them, and plan and split replies end with the next command to paste.

## Analysis

One task can hold both coding and analysis. Use `plan`, `split` and `execute` to build tools and `analyse` to explore data; switch by running the other mode on the same `plan.md`. Phases and analyses share `status.md` and `report.html`.

- **Start**: `/poe:poe analyse <task>`. Without a plan, it writes a short `plan.md` (question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment and how to call `jupyter`, first analysis) and stops for review; if there are decisions only you can make, it grills you first instead of guessing. Then run `/poe:poe analyse poe/<task-slug>/plan.md`. `/poe:poe grill <task>` on an analysis task gives the same plan.
- **Existing plan**: `analyse <plan-path>` on a plan missing any of those fields looks up the facts (data location, environment, `jupyter`), asks you the rest as grill does, writes them into `plan.md` and carries on. The first analysis run is the plan's `First analysis`; later ones are your pick.
- **Each analysis**: a fresh worker adds cells under a heading `## NN: <question>`, runs the notebook headless (`jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace`), writes a record `poe/<task-slug>/analysis/NN-name.md` and appends an entry to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`. It proposes 2-3 next analyses; pick one or say done. Done writes `summary.md`, copies logs and builds the report, as execute does. `NN` continues across runs.
- **Notebook rules**: cells poe writes are tagged `poe:<task-slug>/<NN>` in cell metadata. New cells go into the existing notebook; a new notebook is started only when a full re-run takes over ~5 min or the topic changes, and the record says why. Failed attempts are removed from the notebook and described in the record. Notebooks, figures and intermediates live in the project path from `plan.md`, not under `poe/`.
- **Open notebooks**: `--inplace` rewrites the file, so save your edits before an analysis runs. The worker stops with a warning if the notebook was saved by someone else after it read it.
- **Analysis log**: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` is one chronology for the whole project, across tasks, meant for writing up. Each entry gives the date, question, a one-line result, who did it (poe, user, other agent) and links.
- **Untracked work** (analysis done outside poe, by you or another agent) shows up as untagged code cells. Every `analyse` run starts with a catch-up check (`analyse catch-up` runs it alone): for each group of adjacent untagged cells it drafts a `manual` log entry, asks you to confirm or fix it, appends it and tags the cells `poe:manual` so they are not asked about again. `note <text>` logs such work directly in your words.
- **Well-defined analyses** can also be planned as work units in `split`/`execute`; the worker follows the same notebook rules and writes a record and log entry. Units that write the log run one at a time.
- `report.html` shows analysis records as text; notebooks and figures are not embedded.

Record, log entry and notebook rules in full: [`skills/poe/SKILL.md`](skills/poe/SKILL.md).

## Files

Everything for one task is written under `poe/<task-slug>/` in the directory where Claude Code was started. `poe/` is created if it does not exist. `poe/docs/` is shared across tasks, so `docs` is not used as a task slug.

```
poe/docs/              shared across tasks, written by grill, analyse and note, created when first needed
  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
  ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide analysis log, date order, all tasks
poe/<task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units
  analysis/NN-name.md  one record per analysis
  status.md            state of every phase and unit
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker
  summary.md           execution summary, written at the end of execute/resume
  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts (.jsonl) of each session, including worker subagents
```

Commit all of `poe/`, including `logs/` and `report.html`. Together with the plan, briefs and progress notes, the transcripts are what make an agentic workflow reproducible: anyone can see what was asked, what each agent did and why. The copied logs contain the full conversation, including file contents and command output, and so does `report.html`. Check them for secrets or data that must not leave the machine before pushing, and add only those paths to `.gitignore` if needed. Do not ignore all of `poe/`, so that `poe/docs/` stays in git.

To build the report by hand, without a Claude Code session (needs Python 3.9 or later, standard library only):

```sh
python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>
```

`<plugin>` is this repository, or the installed plugin directory. The script writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. It is a single HTML file with no external resources, so it opens offline.

Tasks created by older versions in `plans/` can be moved with `mv plans/<slug> poe/<slug>`.

## Thinking text in the report

The report can only show thinking that Claude Code recorded in its transcripts. By default it records none, so each thinking step reads "thinking text not recorded". The overview and each transcript's totals show "thinking recorded: N of M blocks", so you can see whether it was on for a run.

To record it:

- Interactive sessions: set `"showThinkingSummaries": true` in `~/.claude/settings.json` (all projects) or in the project's `.claude/settings.json`. This also shows the summaries in the terminal. Workers are expected to inherit the setting from the session that starts them.
- Headless `claude -p` ignores that setting. Pass the flag instead: `claude -p --thinking-display summarized "..."`.

The recorded text is a summary of the reasoning, not the raw reasoning. Sessions that ran without it cannot be filled in later.

Steps labelled "narration" are short progress updates Claude Code writes alongside thinking; they are shown as they are and not counted as thinking.

## Context settings

The 100k figure is a soft target, not a hard cap. A worker nearing it finishes the step it is in the middle of, updates its progress notes, and returns. The orchestrator then starts a fresh worker from those notes.

`phase-worker` sets `autoCompactWindow: 150000`. This is a backstop above the target, so that automatic compaction does not interrupt a step at exactly 100k. It does not cap cumulative usage. Older Claude Code versions ignore the setting without warning.

The `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` environment variable takes precedence over a subagent's `autoCompactWindow`. Setting it to 100000 would make workers compact at 100k regardless, so leave it unset or set it above the target.

## Repository layout

```
.claude-plugin/plugin.json        plugin manifest
.claude-plugin/marketplace.json   marketplace listing this repo as the plugin source
skills/poe/SKILL.md               the /poe:poe orchestrator skill
skills/poe/GRILLING.md            the interview method used by grill
skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py  builds report.html for a task
agents/phase-worker.md            the worker subagent
```

To try local changes without installing: `claude --plugin-dir /path/to/this/repo`.
